The point is that this regulation sets out award criteria, not access criteria. Failure to meet these conditions does not exclude participation in the provision of benefits, but simply sets it in a hierarchy. During the competition it may turn out that someone who meets these criteria will be in the first place, and someone who does not meet these criteria will be in the second place, and one, and the other offer can be accepted by the National Health Fund. And the other thing is, it works in a whole system. It must be borne in mind that the National Health Fund, in the form of a decision by the President of the National Health Fund and in the form of a decision by the director of the National Health Fund branch, identifies the products and identifies the areas in which these services are to be contracted. This is also extremely important, because these areas decide, including access criteria, i.e. basket regulations, and award criteria, i.e. the regulation on which we are proceeding, the possibility of performing or the possibility of winning a contest by a given provider of benefits. So, in fact, first of all, it is important to note that this does not close the possibilities, but these are the bonus criteria. Again, I am talking about this because this is a very important issue in understanding this criterion. In addition, it should be noted that management efficiency will be increased, security of supply will be improved and that this will also be a return to the traditional three-level structure of the company. (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, To answer the first question, whether the government will give up plans to create a National Tax Administration, I will say: the government will not give up because it is one of the elements of public finance recovery. Currently, tax administration, fiscal control and Customs have a distributed structure, as we all know in three independently functioning verticals, and are unable to perform their tasks effectively and are very costly. In relation to other European countries, our costs are 1.6 in relation to the revenue generated, and in other countries it is 0.7 and 0.8. That's what he says for himself. In the tax and customs administration, there is a duplication of certain tasks related to the collection of tax and customs duties within the individual services, for example. checks on the correctness of the implementation of tax and customs obligations. The establishment of the National Tax Administration opens the way to improving the entire tax system and the measures taken are aimed at improving the efficiency and optimisation of structures. Institutional reform is only one element - as I have already said - of the system repair process, in addition to changes in law, e.g. so-called. the fuel package, the amendment of the VAT Act and the amendment of the Act on Gambling, the anti-tax avoidance clause, and the IT tools that are currently scattered are not integrated, and without a common database, we are not able to quickly control those involved in VAT fraud. That is why they have an advantage over us, because within a few weeks they can set up an entity that they then liquidate, and we are just carrying out a check and we find that VAT fraud has been carried out. Reform is necessary in the face of an existing large tax gap, where the rate is 26%. , while, ladies and gentlemen, in the European Union it is on average 15% , e.g. in the Netherlands, 4%. These are the facts that speak for themselves and clearly tell us that we need to make a thorough reform, above all, in the structure, but not only in legislation, which is already being done, and work in this area is going very intensively. With regard to the question, how many customs workers will lose their jobs in the event of the liquidation of the Customs Service, I would like to say that there will be no liquidation of the Customs Service, it is just a transformation into a new structure. All the officers who work at the borders, in mobile groups, that is, those officers who are actually investigating crimes, will find employment as officers, because in the new structure we will create a strong pawn combined with tax intelligence, which will have a tool to deal with really big frauds, not small cases that are expensive and do not have any effect on the budget. The National Tax Administration will be based on existing staff resources, as I said. It is assumed that experienced and substantively prepared officers and employees will find employment in the new structure of the KAS, except for those who will move to their pensions. Ladies and gentlemen, there are about 5,000 of these people, so there is no fear that any of the employees currently employed, contrary to what is claimed, will lose their jobs, will receive job offers. Optimisation of employment can therefore be achieved in full, as I said, naturally, by exempting people of retirement age from employees employed in tax offices, tax offices and customs officials. The Ministry of Finance is interested in the most effective use of existing personnel. This means building on the activities of the emerging service on the knowledge, involvement and experience of those who are already working in the structures of the finance minister. The assignment of tasks to individual persons currently employed in tax control offices, in Customs and in tax administration will be based on the needs of the KAS units and the employment of these persons so far, so as to obtain a modern, uniform and, above all, a very efficient and effective service in the National Tax Administration. As regards pension rights, the acquisition of pension rights in respect of Customs officers, I would like to say that the Council of Ministers is working on a draft position of the Government vis-à-vis the citizens of the bill amending the Act on Pension Supply of Police Officers, Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Military Counterintelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service, Central Anti-corruption Office, Border Guards, Government Protection Office, State Fire and Prison Service and some other laws, this is print No. 30. This draft law aims to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 3 March 2015. The draft position of the Government towards the citizens of the draft law will be the subject of a permanent committee of the Council of Ministers meeting on 9 June. In the opinion of the Government, two models of determination of the right to a uniform pension for Customs officers are acceptable, taking into account only an internship in the Customs Service from 15 September 1990 after the following conditions have been met: at least 55 years of age and 15 years of service in the performance of the tasks referred to in Article. 2 (2) Point 1 (4) (6) of the Customs Service Act in the case of customs officers who have entered the service before 1 January 2013 and the second option is to reach the age of at least 55 years and to work for at least 25 years in the performance of the tasks referred to in Article. 2 (2) 1 Customs Service Act [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the case of Customs officers who entered the service after 31 December 2015 And I would like to say, ladies and gentlemen, that we have no objections, we want to grant these pensions, but to those officers who are actually available 24 hours and who have carried out the tasks referred to by the Constitutional Court's judgment. On the other hand, those officers who have performed normal tasks, like any other employee in the civil service, these pensions, in our view, should not be granted, because this would be in line with the principles of social justice as regards other professional groups. Thank you very much.

As I have declared in several of my speeches, the response will be up-to-date and immediate as necessary. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On 30 April 2016, the Law of 14 April 2016 on the suspension of the sale of real estate of the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury and on the amendment of certain laws entered into force. This law introduced a number of amendments to the rules governing the marketing of agricultural real estate. At this point I would like to remind you that this law, first of all, stopped the sale of real estate belonging to the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury for a period of 5 years from the date of the entry into force of the Act, excluding real estate intended for investment purposes and agricultural real estate with an area of up to 2 ha. In addition, the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development, at the request of the President of the Agricultural Property Agency, will be able to agree to sell property other than those indicated if socio-economic considerations so warrant. Nothing bad has happened in this matter so far. Secondly, the Act of 11 April 2013 on the shaping of the agricultural system has been amended. Firstly, the introduction of the principle that the purchasers of agricultural property may in principle only be individual farmers, namely farmers with agricultural qualifications, who personally carry out at least 5 years' agricultural holding with an area of not more than 300 hectares of agricultural land and who reside on that holding. This requirement will not apply to the acquisition of agricultural property by local government units, the Treasury, ecclesiastical legal persons, religious associations and loved ones. I particularly stress: loved ones, because this problem has also started to appear during this month. The next case, agricultural property may also be acquired through inheritance, except that in the event of inheritance under the will, the agency will have the right to acquire real estate inheritance if the estate heir is a person outside the circle of loved ones but not an individual farmer. With the agreement of the President of the Agency, expressed by an administrative decision against which the appeal will be given to the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development, agricultural property will also be able to acquire other entities providing guarantees of proper agricultural activity, if, at the same time, the acquisition does not result in excessive concentration of land, here we are talking about this limit of 300 ha. In order to eliminate the possibility of speculative purchase of agricultural land by persons whose principal activity in life and occupation takes place outside agriculture and their purchase is treated as a form of investment in capital, there is a ban on the disposal or transfer of agricultural property to another entity without the consent of the court for a period of 10 years. Another case to be recalled, the Agricultural Property Agency was granted the right to buy shares or shares in commercial companies owned by agricultural real estate. In addition, in the event of a change of partner or the accession of a new partner to a partnership which owns an agricultural property, the agency acting in favour of the Treasury will be able to make a statement on the acquisition of that property for payment of the equivalent monetary value. I would like to inform the High House that such circumstances have not yet been established. Further important factors to be said, i.e. the raised doubts, including in Mr Robert Telus's speech, as well as during the work on the bill on the suspension of the sale of the property of the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury and on the amendment of certain other laws - were raised doubts related to the inheritance of the property by persons close to the owner. Agricultural real estate can be acquired through inheritance, except that in the case of inheritance under the will, the agency will have the right to acquire inherited real estate if the estate heir is a person outside the circle of legal heirs - including loved ones, i.e. the successors, the initials, the siblings, the children of siblings, the spouse, the accompaniers and the persons appointed - or the non-individual farmer. Persons who inherit agricultural property, e.g. after parents, they will not have what we confirm, and there were a lot of reservations and questions, the obligation to personally run the farm, which included the agricultural property they inherited. Inheritance of agricultural property for the benefit of a loved one and acquisition of real estate by such a person, e.g. by way of a donation, may take place regardless of the area of this property. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u249	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! [[Besides, it's a poppy fig.]] Ladies and gentlemen! The Constitutional Tribunal is a judicial authority operating for 30 years in the Polish legal system. Its jurisdiction is defined in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland of 1997. 197 held that the organisation of the tribunal and the procedure for proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal were governed by the law. It is currently the Act of 25 June 2015 on the Constitutional Court. Several months of application of this act, already amended several times, shows that the solutions contained therein need to be reviewed. According to § 84 of the Annex to the Regulation of the President of the Council of Ministers of 20 June 2002 on the principles of legislative technique: In view of the above, in view of the numerous legal problems related to the Act of 25 June 2015 on the Constitutional Court, signalled by national and international entities interested in maintaining constitutional order, it is appropriate to draw up and issue a new law in this area. In particular, this need also arises from the fact of a legal dispute involving the Constitutional Tribunal itself. On 9 March 2016, in Case K 47/15 pending before the Court of Justice, the Court, in proceedings in the composition of an unknown constitution and law, reached a decision which does not meet the standards of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. The resulting refusal of publication and further action of the tribunal alongside the applicable law poses a threat to the legal order contrary to the principles of legal certainty and security. The legislative activity, which has the right to rule universally and to regulate the rules of procedure before the Court, can solve this problem by repealing the controversial 2015 act and issuing a new regulation. The practice of the Constitutional Tribunal to enter into force of the 2015 Act, including the resources of organisational experience, as well as of proceedings and decisions, leads to the conclusion that the provisions of the Act of 1 August 1997 on the Constitutional Court, i.e. the Law preceding the 2015 Act, provided that the body with a smooth functioning and proper fulfilment of its powers. The change thus undertaken is formally complete. The law will have a new shape, but in practice it will be easy to accept. After all, it is based on a law well known to the authorities of the State, including the Constitutional Court, because it was ruled on the basis of it several months ago. The law only introduced, I am talking about this 1997 law, a few major amendments, about seven articles, and a dozen partial adaptations of existing standards resulting precisely from the above mentioned at the beginning of the sentence of the amendments, called essential. The designer introduced them in order to improve the process and to improve the rank and legitimacy of the decisions of the Constitutional Tribunal. He also took into account the relevant case law of the Court with them. The draft law on the Constitutional Tribunal is based on four chapters. Chapter 1 concerns the jurisdiction and system of the Constitutional Tribunal. Chapter 2 sets out proceedings before the Court, general provisions, proceedings for the compatibility of ratified international agreements and normative acts with the Constitution and on legal questions, ruling on constitutional complaints, resolving disputes of competence, examining the compatibility of political parties' objectives or activities with the Constitution. Chapter 3 governs the rules and procedures for the judgment and enforcement of judgments, hearings, hearings and rulings of the tribunal. Chapter 4 contains transitional and final provisions. The first provisions of the proposed law refer to the political position of the tribunal resulting from the provisions of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. With regard to the jurisdiction and system of the Constitutional Tribunal, the proposed law characterizes it as a judicial authority and determines its competence by referring to the scope indicated in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland - art. 1, Article Article 2 and Article 2 3 projects. Art. 4 of the project regulates the scope of the information obligations of the Constitutional Tribunal, which the Tribunal carries out in relation to Parliament, i.e. the Sejm and the Senate. This is not only due to the principle of the division of authorities, but also to the mutual balance of authorities. This mechanism consists in the fact that the legislative authority creates acts subject to scrutiny and, if necessary, executes decisions of the Constitutional Tribunal by creating in place of provisions recognised by the Constitutional Tribunal as contrary to the constitution of legal remedies. To provide the Sejm and the Senate with information on the relevant problems arising from the activities and case-law of the Court and the possibility to submit observations on the deficiencies found and gaps in the law, which are necessary to ensure the consistency of the legal system of the Republic of Poland, is also an expression resulting from the principle of division and balance of power of the obligation to cooperate with the authorities laid down in the preamble to the Constitution of the Republic of Poland of 1997. This is all the more important because the Constitution of the Republic of Poland did not confer on the Constitutional Court the power to introduce legal standards into the legal system, in particular the legislator did not authorize the Court to lay down rules of law in general. In the area of constitutional competence of the tribunal, there is also no known doctrine, although strongly debatable and controversial, an institution to revive the old provisions of the law by recognising contradictions with the constitution of the new standards. Art. The 5th draft shall determine the composition of the Constitutional Tribunal and the term of office of the Judges of the Tribunal. The provision provides that the Constitutional Tribunal shall consist of 15 judges, which is a consequence of constitutional norms. The term of office of Judges shall be nine years. Art. 5 of the proposed law also defines the qualifications of candidates for a judge, which clarify the constitutional wording that they are chosen from among those who stand out in legal knowledge, that is, Article. Paragraph 194. 1st sentence and next sentence of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 the proposed law lays down the rules for the submission of candidates for judges, so that this belongs to a group of at least 50 Members or the Sejm Bureau, and the rules for their selection: a resolution adopted by an absolute majority in the presence of at least half of the total number of Members. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The rules and procedures for the taking of the oath by the persons elected as Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal have been laid down in 5 and 6 of the draft. The legislator authorizes the President of the Republic of Poland to receive it. In the text of this article we will also find a rota of oaths and the consequences described both in terms of refusing to take it and in terms of taking it. In the first case, this is tantamount to resigning. The consequence of the taking of the oath is the right to enter into office and, at the same time, the duty of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal to create such a judge's conditions for the taking up of the duties of judge. This is the consequence of the judgment of the Constitutional Court of First Instance. K 34/15, in which we read - quote: The vows made to the President in accordance with Article. Article 21 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Constitutional Tribunal Act is not merely a solemn ceremony of a symbolic nature, referring to the traditional inauguration of the term of office. This event has two important functions. First of all, it is a public promise by the judge to act in accordance with the vows he has made. In this way, the judge declares his/her personal responsibility for the impartial and careful performance of his/her duties in accordance with his/her conscience and respect for the dignity of his/her office. Secondly, the taking of the oath allows the judge to begin office, i.e. the execution of the mandate entrusted to him. These two important aspects of the vow show that it is not just a solemn occasion, but an event with concrete legal implications. For this reason, the involvement of the President in taking the oath from the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal elected by the Sejm should be placed in the sphere of the exercise of the powers of the head of state. The result is an obligation for the President of the Tribunal to ensure that the judge is given the oath of the conditions enabling him to perform his judicial duties. This involves the independence of the judge. The only person entitled to restrict the performance of his duties by the judge shall be a disciplinary court. The subsequent provisions of the proposed Chapter 1 concern the remuneration of the judge. They depend on the average remuneration determined by the Central Statistical Office, using a specified multiplier. They also introduce an institution of retirement after the end of its term of office, regardless of the retirement age. The rules of the judge's operation are based on his independence. Art. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland - followed by Art. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 of the proposed law - states that: "Judges of the Court in the exercise of their office are not bound by the Constitution and are subject only to the Constitution. However, independence cannot be regarded as the subjective right of the judge. It is a quality which determines the substance of the performance of the judge's office and thus guarantees the fairness of the judgment. One guarantee of independence is the institution of immunity. However, immunity is not absolute, but only functional. It is therefore permissible to enter the scope of judicial independence, to the extent necessary, where the aim is to protect the seriousness, authority and independence of the judicial authority - similarly in the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 24 June 1998, sygn. K 3/98. On the basis of the above, Article 2 (1) of the basic Regulation provides that the aid is compatible with the internal market and does not constitute State aid within the meaning of Article 107 (1) of the Treaty. The 7th draft introduces an This is to prevent situations where the judge, relying on his independence and immunity, will act without a legal basis or going beyond the law. Neither independence nor immunity guaranteeing immunity are the institutions to ensure the impunity of the judge or to exclude him from the law. The legislator authorised to waive immunity has been appointed by the General Assembly of the Constitutional Tribunal. Art. The proposed law sets out the responsibilities of disciplinary action and its Article. 9 and 10 are introducing a disciplinary procedure. The following provisions shall determine the composition of the disciplinary court, at first instance, in the composition of 5 judges of the tribunal, in the second instance, in the full composition of the judges of the tribunal, and disciplinary penalties such as reprimand, reprimand, removal from the position of Judge of the tribunal. A general assembly shall be established by drawing lots by the Judges to the formations of the hearing officer. Art. The 11th draft law sets out the grounds for the expiry of the mandate. The termination of the mandate as a result of a decision on the detention of a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal by a disciplinary court was subject to the consent of the President of the Republic of Poland. This solution corresponds to the article. 126 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, in which the Constitutional legislature entrusted the President with the task of upholding the Constitution. It is also related to the statutory position of the President of the Republic of Poland as the subject of taking the oath from the person elected as Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. The President by law becomes the guarantor of the conduct of such a judge according to law and ethics. The President's agreement to terminate the mandate as a result of a ruling by a disciplinary court is also intended to guarantee additional protection for a judge who could be expelled from the service with an abuse of law by a general assembly. It is also worth noting that the absence of a right to be annulled in relation to a ruling by a disciplinary court is balanced. In the event of such a situation, the General Assembly shall act as a resolution to the President of the Republic of Poland and the President shall consent by way of a resolution. Chapter 1 of the proposed law also defines the bodies of the tribunal. According to Art. 12 are the General Assembly and the President of the Tribunal. The General Assembly shall be composed of judges of the Tribunal. The President of the Tribunal shall convene a general meeting at least once a year to discuss the activities of the Tribunal and the problems arising from its case law. The General Assembly shall also have the right to take part in other bodies which, by virtue of their powers, cooperate with the Constitutional Tribunal, i.e. the chair of the parliamentary committees concerned, the Senate committees, the President of the Supreme Audit Chamber, the Minister of Justice, the Prosecutor General, the First President of the Supreme Court, the President of the Supreme Administrative Court, the Ombudsman and the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child. The President of the Tribunal shall notify the President of the Republic of Poland, the Marshal of the Sejm, the Marshal of the Senate and the President of the Council of Ministers, who may also attend the General Assembly or delegate their representative. Art. 14 of the project indicates the competence of the General Assembly. These include the adoption of the Rules of Procedure of the Court, the selection of candidates for the President and Vice-President of the Court, the appointment and dismissal of the Head of the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal, the adoption of the Statute of the Tribunal's Office, the adoption of a draft of the Court's revenue and expenditure, the approval of the information referred to in Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1, performing other activities provided for by the General Assembly in the Act or in the Rules of Procedure. This article also sets out the procedure for the operation of the assembly in the following paragraphs. It shall form a quorum allowing the adoption of resolutions at the level of at least 10 Judges of the Tribunal, which shall constitute 2/3 of the total number of Judges referred to in Article. Paragraph 194. 1 Constitution of 15 Judges. One of those 10 judges must be the President or Vice-President of the Tribunal. The figure 10 represents 2/3 of the figure 15, so this is not an amended level compared to the standard adopted in 1997. The article also formulates voting rules. Resolutions shall be adopted by a simple majority, unless otherwise provided for in the Act. In the following articles of Chapter 1, the draft law defines the powers of the President and Vice-President of the Constitutional Tribunal. In view of the independence of the judges, this role has been limited to the representation of the Court outside and other activities laid down in the Constitutional Tribunal's Act and Rules of Procedure. It also defines the tasks of the vice president and the situations of replacement. The organisational and administrative working conditions of the Tribunal shall be ensured by the President of the Tribunal and by the sub-office of the Tribunal. The President of the Tribunal shall direct the Head, who shall be appointed and cancelled by the General Assembly at the request of the President of the Tribunal. The Head of Office shall be appointed financially at the level of the Undersecretary of State. The rules on the staff of the Court's office shall apply to the staff of the public authorities. There are also issues of budget adoption. The rules of procedure before the Court are laid down in the Constitutional Tribunal Act. In cases not regulated, the law refers to the provisions of the Civil Procedure Code. This is what Chapter 2 of the proposed act treats. One of the basic principles of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal is that it should examine all relevant circumstances in the course of the proceedings in order to clarify the matter comprehensively. In the course of the proceedings, the Court shall not be bound by the conclusions of the parties to the proceedings and may, of its own motion, allow evidence which it considers appropriate to clarify the case. The courts and other public authorities shall provide assistance to the Court and, at its request, submit a file of proceedings relating to the proceedings before the Court. In particular, the Tribunal may request information from the Supreme Court and the Supreme Administrative Court as to the interpretation of a particular rule of law in judicial case law. Such a solution strengthens the principle of independence of the Constitutional Tribunal and promotes the implementation of the principle of material truth, i.e. in the case of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal to determine any circumstances concerning the assessment of the legality or constitutionality of the contested legislative act. The witness or expert may be heard as to the circumstances constituting classified information about the secret classification, or the fact that the competent authority is exempt from the obligation of secrecy. The refusal of consent may be justified only by an important interest of the State. The witness or expert may not exercise the right to refuse to testify if the tribunal considers such refusal to be unfounded. The draft also lays down the rules governing the costs of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal, which prejudges the fact that the costs are borne by the Treasury. Article is relevant from the point of view of the case-law. 25 of the proposed act. It lays down rules for the composition of formations. In discussions on the shape of formations in recent months, it has been argued that when deciding that different cases will be dealt with by the Constitutional Tribunal in various formations, the legislator is obliged to divide cases according to well-chosen and reasonable criteria. Cases which are more complex or concerning the constitutionality of legal acts placed above the hierarchy of legal sources should be recognised by higher formations than those relating to the constitutionality of relatively low-ranked legal acts. The various groups emphasized that the legislator could not divide cases between different formations which ruled freely and arbitraryly, since this is a form of classification of cases recognised by the Court according to their importance and importance. For these reasons, the draftsman has decided to propose that three types of formations be adjudicated: full membership, seven judges and three judges. The criterion for the division of cases between different compositions is of a particular nature. It consists of the gradation of cases recognised by the composition depending on the type of act and the substance of the case under review. In its full composition, the Constitutional Tribunal is to rule on matters of competence between the central constitutional authorities of the State, to establish an obstacle to the exercise of office by the President of the Republic and to entrust the Marshal of the Sejm with the temporary performance of the duties of the President of the Republic, on compliance with the constitution of the purposes or activities of the political party, on the application of the President of the Republic to declare the law compatible with the Constitution prior to its signature or international agreement with the Constitution before its ratification, and on matters of particular complexity. The problem of assessing and recognising the case as a matter of particular complexity leaves the law to the President of the Tribunal. However, it also forms a circle of alternative entities, which may also consider a case to be particularly complex. This is due to the fact that different entities have different positions in the state's system and are guided by other considerations of assessment. Therefore, from the perspective of the tribunal, the formation of the court may find it particularly complicated. Composition of the Court it is also one of those who may find the case particularly complex and request that it be recognised in full. The external entity, which can propose, request full membership, is the President of the Republic of Poland, who is the guardian of the Constitution, so he can demand an assessment of the constitutionality of the problems in full composition, i.e. the more legitimized and representative composition. The projector joined the group of these entities with the Attorney General. The Attorney General may apply to the Constitutional Court in the cases referred to in Article. 188 constitutions. In addition to this power, this body has been a participant in the proceedings before the Court since the beginning of the Constitutional Tribunal, which resulted from successive Constitutional Court laws: 1985, 1997 or 2015. The powers of the Prosecutor-General are linked to the fact that the body is upholding the rule of law and has the task of protecting the public interest. In view of the position of the Prosecutor General in proceedings before the Court, it is appropriate for that authority to request that a case of particular complexity be referred to the full composition. This is supported by the political role of the prosecutor as a public-interest guardian. It should be borne in mind that the control of constitutionality is intended first to ensure order in the legal system and to eliminate illegal acts or parts thereof. This indirectly affects the individual interests of individual entities. In favour of forming the powers of the Prosecutor-General referred to in Article. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of The proposed act provides for exceptions to the obligation to deal with requests in the order of impact at the hearing. Therefore, this act can only regulate internal proceedings in the Court of Justice, which are not regulated by the Court Act. Art. 42 sets out the criteria for the control of constitutionality by the Constitutional Tribunal. In accordance with that provision, the Tribunal, when deciding on the compatibility of a normative act with the Constitution, may examine the content of a normative act or part thereof, or the procedure for issuing it as set out in the Constitution. By proposing such an editorial provision, the projector wants to address the problems arising from cases in which the legislative procedure is contested in the case-law practice. In particular, Article. 42 of the proposal prejudices that the control of the procedure for issuing a normative act is to apply only to the rules resulting from the Constitution. This will avoid controversy over the assessment of statutory and law-making laws. This will also allow the Court to conclude discussions on the allegations raised that, in controlling the legislative procedure, it does not examine the compatibility of lower-rank acts with higher-ranking acts, and it carries out horizontal and even higher-ranking checks on the compatibility of a higher-rank act with a lower-ranking act, which it cannot do under the Constitution. Furthermore, setting a clear reference point in the case of scrutiny of the legislative procedure, such as the Constitution, will promote legal certainty. At the same time, this will be a safeguard against the abuse to date of arguments relating to legislative shortcomings within the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm or the Rules of Procedure of the Senate, which were not based on detailed provisions of the Constitution of the legislative procedure. The draft also introduces two specific issues into the procedure. The first concerns the request by the President of the Republic to the Court to declare compliance with the Constitution of the Budget Law or the Law on the Budget Provision before it is signed. The Tribunal shall then be required to rule on the case no later than 2 months after the date on which the application is lodged in the Court. On the other hand, in cases relating to the finding of conformity of a normative act with a constitution in which a judgment of the Tribunal may have effects relating to financial charges not provided for in the laws referred to in Article. 43, the President of the Tribunal is obliged to request an opinion from the Council of Ministers. The Council of Ministers should issue it within 2 months. The absence of an opinion of the Council of Ministers after the expiry of that deadline shall not hinder the examination of the case. In accordance with the draft, the rules on the determination of the compatibility of ratified international agreements and normative acts with the Constitution shall apply mutatis mutandis to the recognition of a legal question. A separate part of the bill is the ruling on constitutional complaints. A constitutional action may be brought after the expiry of the legal road, provided that this road is provided for, within 3 months of the service of the final judgment, final decision or other final decision to the applicant. The complaint shall be dealt with in accordance with the principles and procedures laid down for the recognition of applications for the compatibility of laws with the Constitution and other normative acts with the Constitution or laws. However, in the case of complaints, the provisions relating to applications relating to their identification in the order of impact shall not apply. It's important, that's what I'm pointing out. In addition to the requirements of the pleading, the action should contain: a precise definition of the law or other normative act on the basis of which the court or public administration finally ruled on the freedoms or rights or obligations set out in the Constitution and for which the applicant seeks the declaration of non-compliance with the Constitution, an indication of what constitutional freedoms or rights and how, in the complainant's view, the grounds for the action were infringed, the reasons for the complaint, stating the precise description of the facts. The action shall be accompanied by a judgment, decision or other decision giving the date of service, based on the contested normative act. An action and a complaint against the order to refuse the application must be made by a lawyer or legal counsel, unless the complainant is a judge, a prosecutor, a notary, a professor or a habilited doctor of legal sciences. The applicants are entitled to establish a lawyer or counsel of their own motion on the basis of the Civil Procedure Code for financial reasons. The complaint shall be subject to prior recognition by law. In the event of a constitutional complaint, the Tribunal may issue the so-called Tribunal. provisional provision. It concerns individual complainants and its purpose is to safeguard the interests of the complainant or of the public interest. It is a provision to suspend or suspend the enforcement of the decision in the case in question if the enforcement of the judgment, decision or other decision could have irreversible consequences, which would be a serious loss to the complainant, or where there is a serious public interest or other important interest of the complainant. That order shall be served without delay on the applicant and the competent judicial authority or enforcement authority. It should be pointed out that the drafter knowingly provided for the possibility of issuing a provisional order only in cases pending on the basis of constitutional complaints, since, in this form of review, the decision of the Court may have an impact on the enforcement of the final judgment in the case. In the abstract review initiated by the proposal, the impact of the judgment of the tribunal on the entity will be only indirect, the audit of the application there is no direct link with individual private entities' affairs. In the case of legal questions, it is not necessary to issue a provisional order, since the case is not yet completed and the decision to be delivered by the court depends on the reply of the Constitutional Tribunal. The Tribunal shall inform the Ombudsman and the Ombudsman of the initiation of proceedings on a constitutional complaint. On the other hand, the Ombudsman and the Ombudsman may, within 60 days of receipt of the information, declare their participation in the proceedings. Art. The 52nd draft provides that the parties to the constitutional complaint proceedings before the Tribunal shall be the complainant, the authority which issued the contested normative act, or the Public Prosecutor's Office of the State Treasury if the Council of Ministers has appointed a prosecutor to represent the Council of Ministers or Ministers in proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal and the Public Prosecutor General. The consequence is also to include it in the catalogue of entities of this article. The hearing shall take place regardless of the appearance of the parties to the proceedings. The proposed law regulates the settlement of a dispute of competence. As a dispute of competence, the law defines a situation where two or more central constitutional authorities of a State have considered themselves competent to settle the same case or have issued a decision in it, or where those authorities have found themselves inappropriate to settle a particular case. This is a development of the powers of the Court as defined in Article. 189 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The Court shall rule on a dispute of competence at the request of one of the authorities. They are also mentioned in Article. 192 constitutions. The proposal should indicate the contested act or omission and the provision of the Constitution or the provision of the law which has been infringed. The initiation of proceedings before the tribunal shall result in the suspension of proceedings before the authorities which have a dispute of competence. In the course of the proceedings, the Tribunal may, after familiarising itself with the position of the parties to the proceedings, also make provision for the provisional regulation of the matters at issue and, in particular, for the suspension of enforcement proceedings where this is necessary to prevent serious damage or caused by a particularly important social interest. The draft law also lays down a procedure for examining the compatibility of political parties' objectives or activities with the Constitution. The conclusions on the constitutional compatibility of the objectives of the political party as set out in the Statute or programme shall be recognised by the Court on the basis and in the manner provided for in the recognition of the conclusions on the compatibility of normative acts with the Constitution. In special situations, the Tribunal shall apply the provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure accordingly. The burden of proving non-compliance with the Constitution lies with the applicant who, for this purpose, should provide or submit evidence of that non-compliance. In the proceedings, the political party shall be represented by the persons appointed to represent the party under the law and the statutes of the party. If it is not possible to determine who represents the party, or when it cannot be contacted, or when there has been a change of the person authorised after the application has been made to the tribunal, the Tribunal shall consider the person actually in charge of the party at the time when the applicant has taken up activities contrary to the Constitution. The Court may also instruct the Prosecutor-General to collect and consolidate evidence to the extent necessary to investigate the compatibility of the political party's activities with the Constitution. The provisions of the Code of Criminal Procedure shall apply mutatis mutandis to this investigation. The scope of the proceedings referred to in the decision of the Court shall be binding. Such an objection is intended to ensure that this entity has the right to assess its ability to provide sufficient time to prepare for the hearing. It will allow him to balance his interests and to consider autonomously whether he prefers to settle the matter more quickly or whether to spend more time preparing himself. The presence at the hearing of the applicant shall be compulsory. In the event of failure of the applicant or his representative, the Tribunal shall either dismiss the proceedings or postpone the hearing. In the event of failure of the parties to proceedings whose presence at the hearing is compulsory, or their representatives, the Tribunal may postpone the hearing and fix a new period for it at the same time. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u269	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club the position on the drafts of the three Constitutional Tribunal laws: civil project, print No 550, the parliamentary draft law on the Constitutional Tribunal, print No 558, and the parliamentary draft law amending the Constitutional Tribunal Act, print No 569. I will start by referring to a project presented by a group of citizens represented by the Legislative Initiative Committee of the Constitutional Tribunal Act. The project was submitted on the basis of Article. Article 118 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 the Constitution and the relevant provisions of the Law on the implementation of the legislative initiative by citizens. This proposal essentially restores the original wording of the law adopted by the PO-PSL Coalition on 25 June 2015. Given the scale of the controversy which was caused by the adoption of that law, this does not appear to contribute to the rapid conclusion of the dispute. However, the proposal also introduces a dozen amendments to the 2015 Act. Some of them respond to the criticism of the solutions of the current Court Act and respond, at least in direction, to the changes expected by the various parties to the dispute. Thus, according to the draft, the General Assembly of the Court would adopt a resolution not by a simple majority but by an absolute majority. The draft assumes the term of office of the President and Vice-President of the Tribunal, and that each member of the General Assembly may vote for only one candidate in the selection procedure. Interesting - although perhaps difficult to implement until the Constitution is amended - there is also a proposal for judges of the Tribunal to elect the Sejm by a majority of 2/3 of votes, as well as detailed rules on situations where this is not possible. There is an obligation to study in full the constitutionality and legality of important politically and socially important laws, in particular budgetary, tax, electoral laws governing the structure and jurisdiction of public authorities, as well as codes. Such regulations are indeed in line with the expectations expressed by more than one of the parties present in the discussions on the model of functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal. Therefore, according to the projectors' declarations, they can be defined as compromises and therefore worthy of consideration and further work. I will now refer to the draft Constitutional Court Act submitted by the group of Members of the Law and Justice. This is a project that can be described as a salomone project. [[Ha!]] It crosses most of the disputes concerning the solutions contained in the Act of 25 June 2015. In view of the deep political divisions that led to the adoption of this law, the adoption of the proposal would contribute to the completion of a significant part of the existing controversy. This is due to the fact that the project essentially restores the provisions of the earlier Constitutional Tribunal Act of 1 August 1997, which was in force between 1997 and 2015. The project is based on a law well known to the authorities of the State, including the Constitutional Court, as it was based on a ruling by the summer of 2015. In other words, the regulations which, first, constitutionality was not challenged by any significant political or social group and which, in any case, compared to the controversy that the 2015 Act is creating, although not always satisfactory, have been in practice for almost two decades. As I mentioned, although the proposal essentially restores the provisions of the earlier Constitutional Tribunal Act, it contains derogations from the provisions in force at the time. I will not discuss them in detail, because it has already been done by the applicant Mr Stanisław Piotrowicz. [[No. Please do it again, go ahead.]] In part, they stem from our belief that the Court's work must be improved, and partly from taking into account the case law of the Court. They strengthen the legitimacy and transparency of the Constitutional Tribunal's activities. It is worth signaling now that some of them are going in the same direction as the proposals for regulation from the other two projects that we are currently debating. The proposed bill does not require a revision of the Constitution, does not resolve all the issues that are currently under discussion, but offers an opportunity to stabilise the situation and reduce the temperature of political dispute. The most recent of these proposals is the draft of a group of Members of the Polish People's Party, which assumes an amendment of the current law on the Constitutional Court. According to the intentions of the projectors, it is consensual and is intended to meet the expectations of all political groups. We estimate that these are interesting proposals, although sometimes against the intentions of designers they may require a change of constitution. The draft provides for the selection of judges of the Tribunal by a majority of 2/3 of the votes, which would depoliticise their choice. An interesting proposal, perhaps at least in part feasible, is that judges, whose validity of the choice exists, be put to rest. It goes beyond the current discussions, but it is worth considering a solution so that the Sejm can ask the tribunal to re-examine the case when new circumstances arise. The proposal that judges should be assigned to cases in alphabetical order, in accordance with the order of influence of the cases, is opposed to the often formulated expectations. In conclusion, it is good that there is agreement beyond one political environment regarding the need to amend the rules governing the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal. There is also agreement that such changes must comply with the existing constitution, be based on social dialogue and be of a compromise nature. We see the constructive value of not only our own project, but also some of the solutions proposed in the other two projects. In matters that arouse so much political emotion and wake them up for so long, a destructive approach seems - everything or nothing. We therefore believe that based on the solutions contained in the proposed projects, optimal, compromise regulation needs to be developed. By acting on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I declare that the club will support further work on all three projects.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u421	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I am pleased to present a draft law amending the Labour Code Act. The proposed amendment aims to extend and harmonise deadlines for employees to appeal to the labour court. For the sake of clarity and conciseness, I would like to point out that, although the proposed amendments to the article. The 264 Labour Code deals with four different situations, namely termination of the employment contract, termination of the employment contract without notice, termination of the employment relationship and refusal of admission to work, and I will discuss them together as rapporteur on account of the identity of the entity and the objectives of the project. I also share the belief that the current diversity of the terms of material law - for short and very short - does not have any justification in today's realities. In 1985, when the article was adopted. 264, the labour courts ruled faster. Ratio legis was based, among others. to assume that the court will be able to deliver a judgment before the termination of the employment relationship and thus settle the conflict situation. In today's reality, unfortunately, it's completely fictional. There is no legal option for this to happen. The proceedings are too long-term. This situation is based on successful attempts to repair, so the premise of the legislator is no longer in place. We are currently dealing with an unjustified variation in the legal situation of workers with whom the contract is terminated in favour of termination. The proposed amendment will eliminate the differentiation of the deadlines contained in the Labour Code depending on the termination procedure. Simplifying the law and increasing its transparency is a very serious added value that we can achieve in parallel with the main objective pursued by the project developers. Art. § 264 § 1 of the Labour Code defines the term of substantive law, not procedural law. Consequently, its expiry results in the loss of the possibility of redress. It shall be taken into account by a court of its own motion, not on a charge, as in the event of limitation. This is an extremely short term for material law. The staff member should not be dependent in so many cases on the goodwill of the court, which may, but may not, restore the deadline on the basis of the article. 265 Labour Code. The unfavorable situation of the employee deepens the amendment of the Civil Procedure Code of 3 May 2012, which speaks of changes. The obvious inability to gather all evidence in most more complex cases results in the risk of omission of claims and evidence submitted by the party at a later stage of the proceeding. Abstracting from legal arguments, such a solution already seems unfair at first glance when an employer who has given notice has two weeks to respond to a lawsuit. Without any legal support and unprepared worker who is often in an emotional state of uproar, he sometimes has twice as much time - sometimes not only to make a decision but also to gather evidence, to select a representative and to formally prepare an action. In the light of the experience of life, a week is a really short time to clean up emotionally after such an experience as losing a job. Let's stand up for ourselves, for example. in the case of an elderly person who is a few years short of retirement age. In the opinion of the Supreme Court, even the initial 14-day period proposed was too short to properly prepare for the action. The proposed amendment is valid in terms of axiology. It increases the protection of the employee - the weaker side of the employment relationship and takes into account non-legal, purely human factors. One of these is the accompanying fear of the loss of work for the ongoing regulation of charges. We know very well how low the savings of the average citizen are. For many of them, especially those with lower qualifications and advanced age, job losses pose a risk of immediate financial problems. In Poland, more than 80% households assess their ability to save any money in a 12-month perspective as negligible or not. The amendment proposed by us is very simple and does not burden the budget in any way. It will benefit the citizen from guaranteeing his right to court. This right has been in harm's way for years, since I would like to remind you that Article. 45 of the Constitution refers to the right to consider the case without undue delay. Regardless of the objective problems affecting the extraordinary length of proceedings (for example, the duration of proceedings from the employment relationship in the first quarter of 2016 at first instance is 313 days, while in the appeal court 90 days) their current duration is unacceptable from the point of view of the needy worker's efficient judicial system. It is worth noting that around 2/3 of the cases before the labour courts end in full or in part with regard to the applicant's claim or the conclusion of a settlement. Most of the lawsuits are therefore completely or partially legitimate. Therefore, since the State has difficulties in fulfilling its obligations properly - because a national obligation to protect it is an integral part of civil law - and ensuring that it is implemented - it is all the more important that the worker should not be given such a short period of time to exercise his rights. However, they have nothing to do with the duration of the proceedings that have been opened for a period of several years. For the first hearing, the plaintiff sometimes waits for more than six months, and at the end of the proceedings before some courts, on average, after several months. The problem I have discussed concerns a very large number of employees. Let us not let them fall victim to political bullying. I ask the entire House, regardless of party membership, to take account of the positive results of the consultations carried out. I would like to remind you here that we have obtained positive opinions from very broad social and professional groups in the course of this project's opinion. In view of the above, I am looking forward to a substantive discussion. I hope that the simplicity of the project will also be correlated with the speed of our proceding over it. The sooner the law is published, the sooner we will cover increased protection of workers, for whom these unfairly short deadlines have not yet completed their course. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u425	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present a position regarding the parliamentary draft law amending the Labour Code Act. The project was included in print No 524. It concerns the extension and harmonisation of the time limit within which workers may lodge labour law actions which are directly linked to termination, return to work or compensation and the request for a contract of employment. The current legal status provides for the possibility of appealing the staff member from the time of proper service of the letter terminating the contract of employment within 7 days. This time limit shall be increased if the employee requests payment of compensation or the return to work, or the establishment of a contract of employment, which shall be 14 days. I would like to point out that these provisions were based on and reflected at the time of their adoption. However, this was done in completely different socio-economic and legal conditions. Labour courts were very efficient in resolving their actions, which often ended before the end of the contract of employment. This justified the short time-limits for appeal and did not interfere with the proper course of the proceedings. We are currently observing the chronicity of cases concerning the termination and termination of employment contracts. They may even last a few years, and the time limits for starting a case may be up to six months. This chronic state of affairs with such a short period of time to bring an action before the labour court is clearly unjustified and unacceptable in today's reality. An additional important argument justifying the need to extend this deadline is the fact that there are situations in which employers effectively deliver letters with notice in pre-Christmas periods or before so-called. It's a long weekend. These treatments further complicate the situation of employees. It should be noted that, as a general rule, after amending the provisions of the Civil Procedure Code, all evidence should be reported at the stage of the application. Failure to do so may result in their omission by the courts. In such a legal provision, a period of 7 days is also a gross injustice for an employee. It should also be noted that a staff member who has not complied with a seven-day period can only rely on the recovery by the court on the principles laid down in Article. 265 Labour Code. In conclusion, there are no obvious grounds for justifying the legal difference in the case of workers who have been terminated for termination and workers with whom the contract of employment has been terminated without notice or where the contract of the employee has expired and who have been refused admission to work. It should therefore be considered that the current 7-day time limit for lodging an appeal against termination of the contract of employment is too short and unduly weakens the position of the worker vis-à-vis the employer. On the other hand, the above arguments address to a large extent all the time limits provided for in Article. 264 Labour Code - including the previous 14-day period - therefore applicants propose uniform extension to 30 days, which will benefit workers' rights. The draft law does not give rise to additional financial implications for the state budget or for local government units. Introduction of the transitional provision in Article. 2 aims to remove doubts as to the impact of the new rules on relations arising before the existing provisions. The Act also applies to the conditions arising before the date of entry into force of the Act if, of course, the deadlines provided for in the current wording of the article have not yet expired. 264. Therefore, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club requests that the project be directed to further work in the Committee on Changes in Codification. Of course, there may still be some additional provisions that need to be changed, but that's all I think we'll do in the process of working in the Committee on Changes in Codification and the Permanent Sub-Committee on Labour Code. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u426	Thank you very much, Member. I will ask Mr Jarosław Porwich to speak on behalf of the Kukiz Club15. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u427	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Labour Code Act. The proposed amendment harmonises the time limits for the recovery of claims by employees by introducing a 30-day time limit for: appeal against termination of the contract of employment; request for the return to work or compensation in the event of termination of the contract of employment without termination or termination of the contract of employment; request for the establishment of a contract of employment. So the change concerns the whole article. 264 Labour Code. In the view of Kukiz15, the draft amendment of the law is as desirable and meets the expectations of the employees. The current deadlines, 7-day and 14-day terms, are too short to implement the labour rights. There are cases of dismissal by courts, for example. appeals against statements only due to failure to comply with the deadline for their submissions. The employee may then in no other way or proceedings rely on the unlawful termination of the employment contract as a condition for compensation claims provided for in the Civil Code. It was just too short a time limit that sanctioned - often illegal - the employer's termination of the employment relationship, prevented the compensation due, or blocked legitimate demands for the establishment of an employment contract. The employee therefore has a strict, extremely short time limit for the recovery of his claims, i.e. the time limit for referring the case to a court of law. If the staff member does not bring an action before the court in due time, all his claims shall be extinguished. In conclusion, the introduction of a uniform 30-day deadline allows an employee in a situation that is highly stressful for him due to the loss of his job, proper preparation for the trial, gathering evidence and taking the advice of a professional attorney: lawyer or legal counsel. This gives the worker much greater comfort when it comes to finding himself in a new and unfavorable situation and does not hit the employer, but only extends the time to exercise his rights. The Poselski Kukiz Club15 welcomes this draft amendment to the Labour Code Act. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u429	Thank you. Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! To begin with, I would like to briefly remind the ratio legis of the proposed revision of the Labour Code. There is no doubt that the time limit laid down for an employee to pursue claims for questioning the termination of the employment relationship laid down in Article. The 264 Code is extremely short. Under this provision, an employee may appeal against the employer within 7 days of the service of the notice of termination of employment. Such regulation dates back to 1985 and has not been amended since, despite numerous systemic changes, also in the socio-economic sphere. The employee is obliged not only to write a letter in just a few days to claim, but also from 3 May 2012, as a result of the amendment, to submit any evidence at the same time, as the subsequent notification may result in the failure of the court. The obligations imposed on the employee are therefore high and require time, especially when it is taken into account that the employee is a weaker party to the legal relationship of the employer - the employee and needs more time to get through the thicket of regulations and prepare in substance to defend his interests. Moreover, compared with the overall deadline for the courts to deal with labour law matters between a few and a dozen months, this 7-day deadline in our assessment is indeed extremely short. It is also worth emphasising that it is not enough that the deadline in Article. 264 The Labour Code is burdensome for workers, it is also a sanction for failing to comply with the statutory deadline, after all, so short, it is harmful and disproportionately severe to the consequences that workers may suffer. 7-day period from Article. 264 The Labour Code is considered a term of substantive law. This, of course, means that, as a rule, there is no possibility for a worker to apply for a recovery period. Therefore, the court dismisses the action and there is no longer a need to consider the validity and legality of the reasons for the termination of the employment contract. In order to illustrate the problem, I will present some statistics on the example of last year. In 2015, 9660 cases relating to termination of the employment contract were brought before the regional court, 4630 concerning termination of the employee's employment contract, and 66 concerning the establishment of employment and compensation. 2951 of these cases, that's almost, ladies and gentlemen, 21% , has been dismissed by the court, including to a large extent because of the lack of justification for the time limit being exceeded. The extension of the deadline for workers to appeal may contribute to reducing the number of remote cases and increasing the effectiveness of the exercise of their rights by workers. The Labour Code is generally considered to be a more favourable instrument for workers and guaranteeing their protection at a high level. According to a study carried out by the OECD on the rigour of regulations in the protection of workers in 2013, Poland has a above-average level of protection of workers. In comparison, the lowest level of this protection is guaranteed by law by the United States, while Portugal guarantees the highest level of protection. In conclusion, Polish labour law is adopted with a visible trend in favour of employees. Although labour law should be flexible and balanced, also taking into account the interests of the employer, there is no doubt that, on this issue, the worker should have a longer period to analyse his legal situation and to prepare his argument fairly with the employer. This change is therefore necessary in order to ensure greater protection of workers' rights. Of course, the Modern Posel Club will support this bill. We request that this draft be sent to the committee's deliberations. I think that from your House there will come a law that will actually respond to such very basic needs of employees in Poland. On the other hand, on this occasion, on the basis of the fact that I have a few seconds left, I would also like to appeal to the Minister, the Government, to establish a new law on trade unions. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u433	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Labour Code Act. The proposed amendment of the Labour Code aims to extend and harmonise the time limits for employees to submit labour law actions relating to dismissal, return to work or compensation and to request a contract of employment. It should be assessed that the proposal is moving in the right direction. It leads not only to improving the procedural position of workers who are party to labour disputes, but also to enhancing the sense of social justice, equality with the law and confidence of citizens in the institutions of the state. Currently, the Labour Code in Article. 264 provides that the staff member may appeal against termination of the contract of employment to the labour court within only 7 days of the date on which the letter terminating the contract of employment was delivered to him. This is a very short time limit which often prevents an employee, who is a weaker party to the dispute, from submitting an appeal within that time limit. The employer has the right to collect evidence longer than a fired employee who has only 7 days to prepare the appeal. It could be risked to say that this provision limits citizens' right to court to some extent. In the event of a request for return to work or compensation and of a request for a contract of employment, the time limit laid down in the Labour Code shall now be 14 days, respectively, from the date of receipt of the relevant notices or from the date of expiry of the contract of employment. These terms are twice as long and, given the certainty of legal trading, seem sufficient. Therefore, it is not justified to extend these deadlines. The proposed amendments provide for the harmonisation of all the deadlines provided for in Article. 264 Labour Code and lengthening them up to 30 days. In our view, it will be sufficient to align the time limits for employees to submit labour law actions relating to dismissal, return to work or compensation and to require that a contract of employment be established for up to 14 days. This will align the rights of the parties to the dispute with the employment relationship. The position of workers and employers should not be differentiated. Consequently, all parties to a judicial dispute should have equal rights, as all citizens are equal to the law. Members of the Members of the Free and Solidarni Circle request that the bill be submitted for further work in the Sejm committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Moreover, today we have talked a lot about trust in you, about the Constitutional Court: if the Constitutional Court is a court of law, which was just said today, then this judgment of the Tribunal - I will tell you immediately what judgment, which of December 2005, should have been put into effect long ago. What am I talking about? I'm talking about the Constitutional Court's verdict, nothing else, and I'm about to read it, just about the deputy. Well, all pensioners who work in companies with collective agreements for mining workers have the same right to a coal depot or its financial equivalent, should have equal protection of that right. 12 December 2005 at 3 p.m. 14 The Constitutional Tribunal recognised the combined constitutional actions concerning the right to provide money for the coal depot. The Constitutional Tribunal held that Article. Article 55 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Law of 26 November 1998 on the adaptation of hard coal mining to market economy conditions and specific powers and tasks of mining municipalities, in force until the date of the entry into force of the Law of 5 December 2002 amending the Law on the adaptation of hard coal mining to market economy conditions and the special powers and tasks of mining municipalities, in part including the return of completely decommissioned mines, is incompatible with Article. Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 in conjunction with Article. 32 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. According to the Constitutional Court, there is no basis for the property rights considered in this case to be differentiated on the basis of any reasonable criteria. Regardless of the fact that this law relates to the earlier employment relationship of pensioners employed in companies of different categories - mining companies and mining companies, it has one source: a collective work arrangement for workers of the mining plant of December 1991, so-called. U.S.P. Therefore, all pensioners who have previously worked in enterprises covered by the PHP, having the same right to free coal, carried out in the form of a coal depot or its financial equivalent, should have equal protection of that right. This order is based on the Constitution. As a result of the amendment of the legislation, the subject matter of protection was limited only to persons from mines completely decommissioned. Thus, pensioners employed in mining works enterprises were deprived of the protection of the law resulting from the UZP. The Constitutional Tribunal held that the amended provision in the part involving the return from mines completely decommissioned is incompatible with the Constitution by arbitrary differentiation of the entity's scope of protection of the property right to the equivalent of the right to free coal. The hearing was presided over by the judge of the Constitutional Court Janusz Niemcewicz, the rapporteur was Judge Mirosław Wyrzykowski, the judgment is final and his sentence is published in the Official Journal of the Law. So if this is the judgment of the Constitutional Court, and I in the Seym have been hearing for many, many days of debate on the Constitutional Tribunal, so I understand, this is a very important issue and the judgments of the Tribunal are extremely important, it is all the more necessary to make these judgments respectable and not differentiate pensioners, not deprive them of their acquired rights. They just need to feel that the state is fair and that no one's taking anything from them. On 22 January, the Sejm passed a law amending the Law on the Functioning of Coal Mining in the years 2008 to 2015 and certain other laws, but did not amend the proposed amendment to the article. 13. We'll be in the committees if the Honourable Sejm directs our bill to the committee, talk about the article. 12 laws governing the granting of free coal in kind financed by a budget grant. Here we will not argue, it is also in committee to agree whether it is a deputy in nature or in another form, it is all to negotiate and to compromise. However, the amendment did not include former mining workers or mining plants entitled to a coal debt under the existing rules, which were taken or suspended by the management boards of coal companies and mining companies. I'm reading from our justification, as I said before. I also talked about the reasons why this was happening. This issue is governed by the amendment proposed in Article. 13, to be bored, I'll say we'll talk about art. 12. Amendment to Article 30a eliminates the time limits for the payment of allowances - deputies - from the budget grant. Of course, the amendment will entail a burden on the state budget. We calculated this at PLN 290 million a year. According to the calculation, the entitlement will be granted approx. 160,000. Retirement and pensioners. So many people are hurt, so many people feel that something has been taken away from them, that their acquired rights have been violated, so this basic justice needs to be restored here. 160,000. Pensioners and pensioners - in the amount of 3 t per year for the price of 600 PLN per tonne, which gives, as we have calculated, 288 million PLN. This will probably be corrected by the Ministry of Finance or the Ministry of Treasury, but that will be clarified in committee if our bill goes to committee. The power would be exercised in favour of a mining undertaking authorised by a budget subsidy in accordance with the same principles as is currently being exercised in favour of former mining workers placed in liquidation before 1 January 2015 and of mining workers placed in liquidation before 1 January 2007 as part of the undertaking referred to in Article.
