ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u453	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the citizens' draft law amending the Law of 7 September 2007 on the functioning of hard coal mining, print No 461. The amendment presented by the applicant's representative concerns the issue of the rights to the coal depot, which in particular in Upper Silesia, at the location of the hard coal industry, raises numerous discussions and is still a very lively topic in the industry's workforce. The tradition of miners' rights to collect part of the payment for underground work in the form of a coal depot is long and extends to the pre-war period. However, in recent years, due to the financial problems of mining companies, the poor situation in the hard coal market due on the one hand to the high costs of coal extraction and, on the other hand, due to the drop in coal prices, there has been a considerable problem with the distribution of coal depots. Each of the coal companies individually decided to resolve the issue of the coal debt, which affected the diversity of access to this specific power, which the company's boards treated as an archaic benefit in form or even as a waste and a heavy burden on the budgets of the coal companies themselves, which were sometimes faced with a collapse. The practice of deputies' boards was to pay at the discretion or receive or suspend these mining benefits. The exercise of this right also takes various forms. Some pensioners are paid out by the ZUS, others are paid in a traditional way. Therefore, the situation in the mining environment in Silesia is increasing because of such diversity in the approach of employers to the issue of payment of deputies. Now the situation is that, for example, SRK - The Company for Restructuring of Mine pays coal sales to pensioners and miners, and fedrian mining companies do not pay this benefit at all. The citizens' initiative to amend the law on the operation of hard coal mining is an attempt to remedy the situation of unequal treatment of former coal workers. The proposed regulation is one way to address the issue of carbon allowances. According to the applicants, it allows for a painful solution to the problem in Silesia, providing a guarantee of equal status of pensioners and miners in this respect, and it does so by reaching the state budget for the funds for the payment of the benefits in question. This solution also aims to relieve the burden on mining companies, which are generally in a very difficult situation, which is also intended to significantly reduce the costs of coal extraction. The issue of granting a budget subsidy for the payment of deputies is included in the provisions amending Article. 13 laws. In turn, in the article. 30a The time limits for paying deputies from the budget grant have been eliminated accordingly. The Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice recommends the High House to refer the citizens' draft law on amending the law on the operation of hard coal mining to further work in the committee of Members. Although the print project No 641 is burdened with a legislative error, in this case the view is that the citizens' initiative must be respected, which has been signed by a number of citizens from the south of Poland who support the claims of former mining workers in terms of their rights to the coal debt. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u465	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the citizens' draft law amending the Law of 7 September 2007 on the functioning of hard coal mining. Coal Deputy is a very old mining authority traditionally granted to both workers and pensioners or miners' rencists. The State is obliged to keep the social agreements concluded and should not change the conditions during their duration without the agreement of the other party. Examples of such unacceptable practices were seen during the PO-PSL governments, when the government, with acceptance of the available then Constitutional Tribunal, robbed citizens of the money collected at OFE and raised retirement age. The proposed law aims to bridge the gap of the Act adopted in January 2015 by skipping mining workers and mining plants with the right to a deputy on the basis of the existing legislation, which has been removed by the Boards of Coal Companies and Companies, and to abolish the limitation of the temporary budget subsidy for deputies. Unfortunately, the amendment adopted in December 2015 did not correct and even deepen the gap arising from the lack of regulation of the coal debt. The adoption of this amendment will lead to the harmonisation of the payment of the coal debt from the state budget, by regulating the issue as a whole, while restoring a sense of social justice by fulfilling the commitments previously made by the State. In the current situation, regardless of the legislative flaw that the authors of the project have not avoided, expressing their respect for the initiative related to the collection of 100,000. signatures under the project, Members of the Free and Solidarni Circles request that the citizens' bill be sent to work in the Parliamentary committees, during which an assessment of the impact of this regulation on the state budget should also be carried out, which should be relevant to the determination of the amount due to the eligible coal deputy. Thank you. [[Applause]]

In this context, it is justified to postpone the date of entry into force of the Tax Administration Act of 1 July 2016 to 1 January 2017, which will ensure preparation for the consolidation of the tax administration, customs and tax control units in order to effectively and functionally carry out the tasks of the national tax administration. The Public Finance Commission rejected the proposal to reject the draft law at first reading when discussing this project. The Commission also adopted one legislative amendment to change the title of the project. It is no longer a draft law amending the law on amending the law on tax administration, which is a law amending the law on amending the law on tax administration and the law on amending the law on tax administration and certain other laws. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u579	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The need to adopt the proposed Act on the National Media Council is due to the temporary nature of the Act of 30 December 2015 amending the Act on Radiophony and Television. The legislator made a clear statement to this effect in his article. 4 of that law, indicating 30 June 2016 as the last day of its validity. The need for an urgent new regulation in the field of a standardized December Episcopal Act is therefore the result of a decision by the legislator himself. In the above law, whose ratio legis is laid down in the justification for its project, Parliamentary printing No 158, the legislator indirectly indicated the directions for future reform of public media. First of all, at the root of the deprivation of the National Radio and Television Council's prerogatives as regards the appointment of a part of the composition of supervisory boards and the appointment of members of the boards of public broadcasting companies, there was a negative assessment of the merger in a single authority of the power regulator of the electronic media market, which should be impartial to all participants in that market, with the function of the authority exercising authority in the field of ownership supervision towards entities representing State ownership. Only this first function - the controlling regulator of the media market - is based on the constitutional status of the National Radio and Television Council. According to the Constitution, the National Council: upholds the freedom of expression, the right to information and the public interest in radio and television, as indicated in paragraph 1. Article 1 213. These tasks apply equally to public and private media. The Constitution does not provide for any specific powers of the National Council vis-à-vis public media, but provides for the issuing by the National Council of normative acts and individual acts addressed to unsubordinated entities. The legislator therefore assigns to the National Council the role of a government-independent regulatory authority which exercises public authority over non-subordinated broadcasters. Secondly, by introducing a simplified procedure for the appointment and dismissal of supervisory boards and boards of companies by the Minister responsible for State Treasury matters, reducing the number of members of supervisory boards to three and without establishing the term of office of the bodies of the company appointed in this simplified procedure, the legislator assumed that legal status and the manner in which the authorities of the regulated organisational units were created would change from 1 July 2016. Since the essential elements of the reform under preparation may require a notification procedure in accordance with European Union law and this procedure will certainly not be completed before 1 July 2016, there is an urgent need to adopt the proposed law because, for the reasons indicated above, it is not possible to adopt the whole package introducing national media reform already now, the package contained in the draft laws on national media, Parliamentary printing No 442, and audiovisual contribution, printing No 443, and introductory provisions, printing No 444. Until its adoption Whereas it is necessary to anticipate, with effect from 1 July 2016, the following elements of national media reform: Firstly, the establishment of the National Media Council as a cadence, fully independent of the executive authority and politically pluralist body, which will carry out legal activities concerning the structure and composition of public bodies of radio and television broadcasting, with a view to their thorough fulfilment of their statutory tasks and to protecting their editorial independence and independence from the executive authority in terms of content and form of transmissions. Secondly, granting the National Media Council powers to carry out certain activities which the December Episcopal Act temporarily granted to the Minister of the Treasury, while maintaining a formal, non-influenced role of the Minister as a representative of the State Treasury in general meetings of 20 companies covered by the proposed regulation, until their statutory transformation into non-corporate state legal persons and the creation of the National Media Fund. The six-year term of office of each of the five members of the Board and the ban on joining the Council with employment in the field of executive authority provide an institutional guarantee that public media will be completely independent of the executive authority in terms of both personnel and programme. The composition of the National Media Council, as sui generis of the ownership body, guarantees for the first time in the history of Polish public media the participation of representatives of the parliamentary opposition in the direct exercise of this supervision, including in making important personal decisions. Three members of the Council will be appointed by a majority of the Sejm, and two - the President of the Republic will only be appointed from among the pairs of candidates designated by the opposition sejm clubs if these clubs wish to exercise the right to present the head of state to their candidates. The importance of this innovation cannot be overestimated from the point of view of democratic control of public media and transparency of personal decisions. In the logic of public media reform, the separation of the functions of the sovereign and impartial regulator of the media market from the functions of media ownership, initiated by the December law and confirmed by the proposed regulation, justifies the reduction of the statutory number of members of the National Radio and Television Council, starting from its new term of office, from five to three, including the resignation of Vice-President of the National Council. Constitution in Article. 214 does not specify the number of members of the national council, leaving this to ordinary legislation. In the doctrine of constitutional law, the number 3 is rightly considered to be the smallest sufficient number of members of the national council due to the principle of tres faciunt college and the constitutional requirement for this body to be composed of at least one member by the Sejm, the Senate and the President of the Republic of Poland. The proposed law does not change the public media funding system. A proper change will take place in the framework of the reform under preparation. The creation of a new system for financing national media will be the next step. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u594	Thank you, sir. The President of the National Radio and Television Council, Mr Jan Dworak, asked for the floor. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u595	Mr. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! I wanted to start by saying that it is better late than never, but I will not dwell on the time of our debate because it has already been discussed in club speeches. Let me just say that the rush and the pressure of time are certainly not the way to prepare a good law. The current project presents a vision of changes that are far more cost-effective, without ideological ideas, which - I am convinced of this, like many other observers - would lead to difficult to predict effects, and certainly to confusion, or maybe chaos, in public media. Such concerns were expressed, among others. outstanding media experts and experts gathered at a conference devoted to the analysis of previous draft media laws, organized by the National Council and the University of Warsaw at the end of May in Warsaw. Speaking about the previous project in the High House, I formulated an assessment that the project proposes a model of centralised, politicised and paternalist public media. The current legislative proposal does not allow this opinion to be changed because it is behind it that vision of the country to which these media are intended to serve. Paternalism manifests itself in two ways. The first is a way of proceding this law, as before, in hiding from the public, without public consultation, without the possibility of a free, broad debate - a debate, which today is simply a feature of a well-functioning democracy. Paternalism is also visible in the vision of the media: very traditional, not social, not based on the development of modern technologies, which provide partner communication between the sender and the recipient. This model, which is presented here, is simply a transfer of what was in the past, and we live in a completely different media world. The media, including regional media, are centralised in this project, subject to much stronger supervision than today by the National Media Council, stronger than today. You can clearly see the Hungarian model here. In 2011, the parliament set up a media mission and property fund, MTVA, which oversees two televisions: Hungarian TV and TV Diuna, or actually Duna, Hungarian radio and information agency. Only that the difference between Hungary and Poland is that Hungary had to reproduce the model of public media after a great collapse, and in Poland - with all its weaknesses and disadvantages - public media occupied and still occupy an important part of the electronic media market. Hungary is other public media, their history of recent years is different and another tradition, a smaller country, not such a country as Poland - and I will come back to that for a while. And finally the third adjective - politicisation. It hasn't changed either. It involves taking over media power directly by the politicians of the parliamentary majority. It's the model that's actually in the back of all these changes, which is visible in this bill, I don't know what to call it, second personage or episode. This model should be subject to a comprehensive and serious social debate involving social and creative organisations, not only by the state institutions, because, as I said, it does not agree with the history of the newest Polish media and, more importantly, it is not compatible with the acquis, where the self-government of the regions plays a very important role, nor does it take into account technological changes, how the media is used by the especially young generation. With all these model reservations, the current project needs to be given justice. In relation to previous projects, applicants do not push such a significant number of legal violations, starting with infringements of the basic law. However, we still see in this proposal proposals for changes which are contrary to the existing regulations, including the Constitution. That's all that matters. Firstly, the role of the National Radio and Television Council as a constitutional body of the State is still limited. The current draft law, like the previous one, provides for the repeal of the article. Article 6 (6) (a) 2 point 1 of the Act on Radio and Television requiring the National Council to design in agreement with the President of the Council of Ministers of State Policy in the field of Radio and Television. Why these competences are being repealed, this is not understandable. The development of audiovisual policy concerns the entire media market for which the constitutional body of the national council is responsible for the design and development of the media market. I talked about this extensively in discussions on previous projects. The powers of the National Council indicated in the Constitution and in the Radio and Television Act support the recognition of the National Council as the body competent in shaping State policy in the field of radio and television. It is in the public interest that the position of the National Council should be taken into account in the course of work relating to this sphere of public life. Repeal of the Article Article 6 (6) (a) Point 2 (1) limits not only the competence of the national Council to create state policy, but also the capacity of the various governmental bodies and other public institutions to use the information resources of the national Council and to involve it in the implementation of socially important actions. With the information gathered and developed on the media, only the National Council of Radiophony and Television is adequately prepared to support the Minister of Culture and National Heritage and the Minister of Digitisation in preparing the positions of Poland in the European Commission's work on media and some aspects related to electronic communications issues. This is particularly incomprehensible when the work on amending the European Audiovisual Directive strengthens the position of regulators in national and European forums. Here it is as if we were going in a completely opposite direction than this trend, which is very clear. The Commission has recently published a proposal to amend audiovisual law and there is very clear emphasis on strengthening the role of regulators such as the National Council. A special organisation has been set up, ERGA, which is an advisory body at the European Commission's high level. However, this draft amendment unfortunately greatly limits the role of the national council on the whole market. Secondly, the powers of the National Media Council vis-à-vis supervisory boards are unclear. The December Amendment Act gave the Minister of the Treasury the authority to appoint companies, which triggered a strong and legitimate protest at home and abroad. The current project transmits the appointment of company bodies from the powers of the government body to the National Media Council, whose members no longer need to be experts in the field of media as previously recorded, but only have to distinguish themselves with the knowledge of company law as it is recorded in the current project. Since the project does not exclude the possibility of combining the functions of a member of the National Media Council with certain types of political activity, it will be directly involved in the political trend. Member of the National Media Council, this too has already been mentioned in this Chamber, can be an active politician, supervise public media and at the same time hold a parliamentary mandate. In most European bodies supervising the performance of tasks carried out by public media, it is prohibited to combine these functions. In some countries, this also applies to the exercise of the mandate of a member of the European Parliament, of local Parliaments, of a judge, of a prosecutor, of a civil servant. It should be stressed that the proposal does not contain transparent regulation on matters of competence. The proof of this is, among other things. the proposed provision on the participation of members of the National Media Council in the general meeting of shareholders, i.e. the Minister of Treasury. In what capacity? The project doesn't specify that. It should be stressed that the proposed law does not provide the legal basis for the responsibility of the members of the National Media Council for the actions taken. Doubts about the interrelationship between the National Media Council and the Minister of Treasury, acting as the General Court, are compounded by the proposal contained in the article. 2 of the project, de facto being a modification of the regulations contained in k.s.h. and relating to companies: the Council carries out legal activities concerning the structure and personal composition of bodies of 20 companies of the Treasury, public broadcasting units and PAP, with a view to their thorough fulfilment of their statutory tasks, as well as protecting their editorial independence and independence from executive authorities in terms of content and form of transmission. I would like to draw attention here to the article. Article 14 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2, which refers to the fact that the companies' authorities operate in the composition existing on the date of entry into force of this Act until amendments are made pursuant to the Act referred to in Article. 10 or 11 or amended articles of association. The person appointed as a member of the company's body for a certain term of office may be revoked before the end of his term of office only for the reasons set out in the provisions of the law, very hard, we know these reasons, or in the provisions of the statutes in force on the date of appointment. This would have led to the conclusion that the National Media Council would be able to appoint new authorities, for which it is actually called - it is also about some changes in the statute, but the main function is to appoint public media authorities - it will only be able to do so after the end of the term of office of the current authorities, i.e. after 3 or 4 years. Why should this National Media Council be established now that the most important functions of the National Media Council can only be performed in three or four years? The independence of public media has been repeatedly addressed by the Council of Europe. I would just like to remind you that the Council of Europe, in the declaration of the Committee of Ministers of 15 February 2012 and in the recommendation of the Committee of Ministers to the Member States on guaranteeing the independence of public broadcasters, states that public media should remain independent of political or economic influence, aiming at the highest standards of independence, objectivity and justice. Art. 3 the current legislative proposal states that the members of the Council are independent in the performance of their functions, serving the public good. But how do we really reconcile this with belonging to a political party, to parliamentary clubs that are based on, well, we know, completely different logic? It is also doubtful that the provision on the appointment of the President of the National Media Council is in line. I'm sorry, this time it's about reducing the number of members on the national council. It raises doubts about the appointment of one member by the president. This is a constitutional question, because according to the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the implementation of Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 on the financial rules applicable to the general budget of the Union and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 on the Financial It is very clear there, so it is said that the President can only appoint one member, contrary to the wording of the article. 144, where the plural is used in the recipe. It is therefore doubtful that the President should appoint only one member of the National Council, with its membership reduced to three. Finally, I would like to say that the role of the Senate is unclear because, as you know, the members of the National Media Council are only called by the Sejm and the President, the Senate is excluded from this function, and according to the provisions of the Act it is to receive, it is to be addressed to the report of the National Media Council. It is not known why it should receive this information if it does not participate in the appointment of members of the National Media Council and does not impose constitutional obligations on it concerning radio and television. The draft law contains legislative shortcomings, including, but not limited to: I will not bore the High House with these examples. The national council has had time since morning to prepare its observations. We've actually been working on this case since this morning. It's only hours. From here, I've only focused on the most important things. As far as possible, we will provide the Sejm with more precise analyses as possible. But at the end of the day, I wanted to say something else, and I wanted to say something about what isn't here. There is no change in the way the media is financed. We have assurances that such a way of financing the media will be developed soon, but I would like to remind you that these 2.5 billion PLN, to be exact, which is needed by the media according to the National Radio and Television Council - we know that there are different estimates - this is the possibility of decommercialisation. In fact, this is the only significant positive change that I would personally promise myself after the changes that have been announced for a year now. Without this, without this change in funding, there is no way to think about the real reform of public media. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u639	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The House has made four amendments. The amendment implements EU regulations into Polish law in order to protect registered regional products more effectively. It amends the procedure for submitting applications for registration so-called. protected designations, protected geographical indications and traditional specialities guaranteed. The time limits for examining applications for registration are to be reduced. The Act complements the catalogue of tools for supervising the activities of certification bodies. The duties of the chief inspector of the commercial quality of agri-food products and the principles of cooperation with the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection shall be clarified. The Act requires the principal inspector of the commercial quality of agri-food products to publish on the website a list of producers holding a valid quality certificate confirming the conformity of the production process of the agricultural product or foodstuff with the specification, or a valid certificate of conformity. The amendment provides for the replacement of criminal rules on liability for incorrect use of registered administrative names with financial penalties. One of the Senate amendments eliminates the wording which stresses the need to submit an application in a single copy. It further clarifies the provisions which impose a specific sanction for the redecision of re-marketing of an agricultural product or foodstuff which has not been labelled or marked in breach of the rules. The others are editorial and precise. The Act shall enter into force 14 days after the date of publication.

According to the Senate, this provision is too restrictive, and the Senate has therefore accepted Amendment No 3, according to which, if the Minister responsible for Agriculture agrees, the President should be able to work with another employer. According to the Senate, such a solution will provide sufficient protection for the interests of the Polish Horse Racing Club, while at the same time allowing the President to provide another, socially useful work, e.g. teaching or scientific, as indicated in the explanatory memorandum. Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle share the positive opinion of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development in the scope of the amendments introduced by the Senate of the Republic of Poland, which will have a positive impact on the functioning of the Polish Horse Racing Club. In the opinion of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, these amendments represent an inherent aspect of the Senate's efforts to improve the quality of its law. In particular, it is worth noting the amendment of paragraph 2 of the Senate resolution, where the various dates of entry into force of the Act in question and the Act of 13 May 2016 amending the Public Procurement Law and certain other laws were rightly pointed out, which would result in a conflict between the Law on Government Administrations and the Law on Public Procurement for a certain period of time. The Senate therefore proposed to delete the article. 3 of the Act in question and the transfer of its content to a major novelty of the Public Procurement Law, so that all changes related to the change of competence in the supervision of the President of the Public Procurement Office enter into force within a single period of time. The amendments contained in paragraphs 3 and 4 of the Senate resolution, which contribute to the harmonisation of the terminology used in the amended laws and the precise dedication of the intention of the legislator, are also reasonable in fulfilling the principles of correct legislation contained in the regulation of the President of the Council of Ministers on legislative technique. On the other hand, on paragraph 1 of the resolution modifying Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3a and 3d of the Act on Export Insurance Guaranteed by the State Treasury is of vital importance the doubts indicated in the justification of the Senate resolution, namely what the legislator understood under the concepts of the ability of the Management Board of Export Credit Insurance Corporation to act and adopt important resolutions. As the Senate points out, these concepts are unknown to the Commercial Companies Code, which is subject to corporate rules as a public limited company. Therefore, the Senate assumed that the legislator's will was that the hull board, i.e. having fewer members than that indicated in the provision, it was not de facto the company's body and therefore could not adopt binding resolutions, in particular as regards the submission of declarations of intent, the representation of the company, but merely had the right to conduct its cases. Taking into account the report of the Senate Board, the Free and Solidarni Circle will support the Senate's amendments to the Act amending the Act on Export Insurance Guaranteed by the State Treasury and certain other laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u718	Declaration by the Members on the 93rd anniversary of Jerzy Miatkowski's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 8 June, the 93rd anniversary of the birth of Jerzy Miatkowski, alias Zawad, lieutenant, soldier of the Home Army, member of the Association of Freedom and Independence, one of the enchanted soldiers. Jerzy Miatkowski was born in 1923. He was the son of Joseph and Janina from the House of Garbage. He graduated from the fourth grade of junior high school. At the age of 20, he joined the Home Army. During the Warsaw Uprising as a member of the Lt. Tadeusz was involved in battles in the Śródmieście region, which resulted in Stalag 344 Lamsdorf in the German POW camp in Łambinowice. He was later evacuated over the Rhine, where the entire transport was liberated by the U.S. military. This allowed him to return to Poland in December 1945. At the beginning of the new year Miatkowski, adopting the pseudonym Zawada, was sent to the branch of Jan Szacillow, a pseudonym Renek, belonging to the branch of Jerome After a few months, the latter appointed him his aide. Miatkowski has been with him ever since. In the spring of 1947 at the time of the so-called. The amnesty for the independence underground of Zawada appeared, was vacationed and moved to Warsaw with his parents. However, despite all propaganda, amnesty was not an act of goodwill, but a way to eliminate organized resistance against government opponents. When it was stopped, surveys with names, aliases, and branch information were immediately examined, and the arrests of those who were exposed were followed. They were accused of continuing their anti-state activities. For this reason Miatkowski and a group of soldiers of Major Dekutowski tried to escape to the West. Unfortunately, treason and insidiously organized by the UB of Katowice the provocation led to the arrest in Nysa. Zawada, who was arrested on 15 or 16 September 1947, was sent to the central prison of the MBP in Mokotów in Warsaw. A year later, Miatkowski was sentenced to death in November. The judges of the Military District Court in Warsaw gave their judgment: Major Józef Badecki, Captain Józef Kantecki and Kpr. Richard Wasilewski. The Supreme Military Court upheld the sentence, and President Bierut did not apply the law of grace. On 7 March 1949, Zavada was executed. His fate was shared by comrades Jeronim Dekutowski, Roman Groński, Stanisław Łukasik, Tadeusz Pelak, Edmund Tudruj and Arkadiusz Wasilewski. The lieutenant's body was buried secretly by public security. They were only found in 2012. Links of the Military Cemetery in Warsaw Powązki. The identification of the corpse was carried out within the framework of the project of searching for burial sites and identifying victims of totalitarianisms. Zawada was rehabilitated by the Military Court in Warsaw in the first half of the 1990s. On 15 November 2007, on the 59th anniversary of the death sentence of 7 soldiers cursed by the order of the President of the Republic of Poland Lech Kaczyński, Jerzy Miatkowski was awarded for outstanding merits for independence of the Republic of Poland with the Officer's Cross of the Order of the Rebirth of Poland. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-09-sejm-20-2.u719	Request to restore the image of the national emblem of August 1919 Dear Messengers, Gentlemen! High Izbo! In 2016 we celebrate the 1050th anniversary of the Polish state. On this occasion thousands of Poles, with the highest nobles of the Church and representatives of the Polish state, took part in the April jubilee celebrations. The 1050th anniversary of our homeland is an excellent, unique opportunity to restore the image of the state emblem of 1919. Let's remind you, On 1 August 1919, the Legislative Sejm adopted the image of the emblem in such a way as to refer to the beginnings of the Polish statehood and, what is very important, to the times of our power and glory, especially the times of the Jagiellonian dynasty. The emblem was a white eagle on a red background symbolizing the royal power that united the nation. On the head of the eagle there was a crown crown crowned with a cross, which directly refers to the character of the beginnings of our statehood, entering the circle of Latin culture. The crown with eight sticks descending centrally into the center formed together with the cross a closed whole, which in its symbolism was a sign of our national sovereignty and emphasized the independence of the state. I will mention that military and naval eagles were modelled on the motives of the emblem's image, and the mint was minting coins. This beautiful, proud eagle with a crown and a cross was visible everywhere: on documents, seals, signs of institutions and offices of various levels - central and local authorities. The Polish eagle from 1919 remained only until 13 December 1927. He was replaced by others. Differences: from the crown the cross was removed, two five-pointed stars were added expressing in symbolism the subordinateness of other interests, and the crown was opened, which is a sign of a non-sovereign state, dependent on foreign influence. Dear Messengers, Dear Members, As representatives of the Polish people Let us restore national pride and symbols of sovereignty to Poles and Poland. May the Eagle of 1919 convey to the world the desire of Poles to establish a state whose real shape will be consistent with its symbolism.
